# Cloud Console Hardening — epic charter

Successor to the Cloud GUI Remake epic (SEALED 2026-07-21, PR #5226). Inherited 58 re-parented
survivors + 10 natively-filed rows = 69 open children. This file is the epic's memory: every wave
reads it first.

## Vision

**The console stops lying.**

Nearly every row worth building names a place where the console tells somebody something untrue.
It tells the network 40 requests are 5. It tells every user their session came from `172.18.0.1`
(measured: 48 of 49 production session rows). It tells the access log a bearer token is part of a
URL. It tells the rate limiter all users are one user. It tells a HEAD prober a session token in a
`location` header. It tells the developer a revoke succeeded on a route the mock never called, and
that a CSS check passed on code it just deleted.

After this epic, what the console SHOWS and what it DOES match — and the instruments asserting that
match are themselves able to fail.

This is also the epic's **triage predicate**: a row survives if it names a claim/reality divergence;
it closes when the divergence is gone. That single predicate generates all three movements, because
a lying harness and a lying rate limiter are the same defect class pointed at different audiences.

## Standing laws

0. **A wave may not end with more live rows than it began, and instrument residue is filed under the
   SUCCESSOR, never here.** Added wave 12 (D146). This epic keeps rows whose divergence is visible on
   a console surface a person can reach; every gate, generator, harness, required-checks or
   ledger-hygiene row belongs to `bp-cloud-console-instruments-charter.md`. **That destination is the
   TASK `cch-instruments-epic`, not the file** — the charter document is stranded on unmerged PR
   #8500 while the successor epic task is live, published and carrying 73 children, so file with
   `parent_id: cch-instruments-epic` AT CREATE TIME (legal on every constraint at once: it is a
   create, never a re-parent, and it touches Postgres, not #8500's branch). **RESIDUE MEASUREMENT
   PROTOCOL**: count live rows from the seal predicate's own roster read at FIRST CLAIM and again at
   DEBRIEF; a wave that nets positive states the number and names what it repays. `bp task get`'s
   `child_count` is a LIFETIME total on both epics (a cancel does not decrement it) and its open
   count includes `drafts.*` — at wave 16's first claim the predicate read **196 published children:
   112 done / 65 open / 18 cancelled / 1 considering**, while `bp task get` said 200/67. Quote 65.
   The two halves are one law: without the filing half, the split is undone by the next wave's own
   residue. **Live-row counts
   are quoted from the seal predicate's OWN denominator** — its roster read returns published children
   only, so `drafts.*` entries are duplicates, never rows (D105), and a census that counts them
   overstates. **And a review owed before merge is FILED AS A ROW** — that law was written in wave 9,
   kept only as Paper prose in waves 9, 10 and 11, and was 0-for-3 until wave 12 filed
   `cch-w12-bl-independent-review-owed-wave-12`.

1. **Cite MERGE SHAs, never branch SHAs.** `origin/main` is a linear squash chain, so
   `git merge-base --is-ancestor <branch-head> origin/main` always false-negatives. Get the SHA from
   `gh pr view <n> --json mergeCommit`. Inherited from the GUI epic; re-proved this wave (five
   worktree branches read NOT-merged while their PRs were MERGED).
2. **No row closes without a reproduction attempt.** The false-done pattern (11 tasks fake-done,
   then reopened) is this repo's most expensive recurring defect. A merge SHA plus a `file:line` is
   the minimum evidence.
3. **The census is L4, not L1.** The epic description carries a six-band disposition census written
   at seal round 10. It is a *hypothesis ranking*. Wave 1 measured it: roughly one third was stale —
   always in the SAFE direction (rows more done than claimed, never less). Keep the bands; re-derive
   truth values before building.
4. **Roster reads have exactly two safe routes.** `GET /v1/tasks?filter[parent_id]=…` silently
   returns foreign rows with a 200 (re-proved 2026-07-21: 0 of 15 rows in a default page actually
   carried the requested parent) — never source a roster from it. Use `bp task get <epic> -o json` →
   `.children` for the roster, and a per-row `bp task get <id>` for evidence. `.children` carries no
   `updated_at`/`closed_at`, so it cannot answer close-time questions. `bp task ls` has no
   `--parent` flag.

   **Wave-2 correction — the `/v1/data/query` bracket story was wrong.** Wave 1 recorded that bare
   curl with unencoded `filter[parent_id]` brackets returns "200 with 140 rows from another epic".
   Executably refuted: that command never reaches the server. curl's own URL-globbing parser rejects
   `[parent_id]` as a malformed range and **aborts client-side with exit 3 (`bad range in URL`)**,
   writing no output file. Under `curl -g` or Python urllib — where literal brackets really do go on
   the wire — `/v1/data/query/production/task` filters **correctly** (91/91 rows, matching
   `.children` exactly). The `140` traces to `gr-p5r5-successor-seal`'s roster-delta note for the
   *predecessor* GUI-Remake epic. The real hazard on this route is a **stale-file misread**: a script
   that reuses a fixed output path and skips the exit-code check reports yesterday's bytes as a live
   200. So the rule is not "encode the brackets or get wrong rows" — it is:

   ```bash
   # Route A (preferred): bp task get <epic> -o json | .children
   # Route B (raw HTTP): -G --data-urlencode, fresh mktemp path, and CHECK THE EXIT CODE.
   OUT=$(mktemp)
   if curl -sf -G -H "Authorization: Bearer $TOK" \
        'https://guerrilla.barkpark.cloud/v1/data/query/production/task' \
        --data-urlencode 'filter[parent_id]=cloud-console-hardening-epic' \
        --data-urlencode 'limit=500' -o "$OUT"; then
     python3 -c "import json; print(json.load(open('$OUT'))['result']['count'])"
   else
     echo "FETCH FAILED (curl exit $?) — \$OUT was NOT written by this run; do not read it" >&2
   fi
   ```
   The bracket-abort is generic to curl, not to `parent_id`: `filter[lifecycle_status]` and
   `filter[type]` reproduce it identically.

7. **A gate's classification is a claim like any other — write it, never cite it.** Wave 2 caught
   itself asserting "per the repo's standing advisory classification for Vercel" when
   `grep -in vercel docs/ops/merge-gates.md` returns nothing and
   `gr-blk-vercel-checks-ungoverned` is 0/2. Asserting a rule that does not exist, inside the epic
   whose frame is "the console stops lying", is the same defect pointed at ourselves. If a gate needs
   a classification, land the sentence.
5. **MERGE-GATED criteria are stamped by the LEAD, never the builder.** `autostamp_merge_gate`
   (`api/lib/barkpark/tasks/close.ex`) only fires on a criterion literally carrying
   `merge_gate: true`; this epic's criteria do not carry the flag (systemic gap, tracked in
   `pds-bl-merge-gated-criteria-carry-the-flag`, out of fence). The lead pastes the merge SHA by hand.
6. **Never branch from the primary checkout.** `/Volumes/SATECHI/github/barkpark` is shared by many
   concurrent sessions, is routinely ahead/behind origin, and at the time of writing carried an
   uncommitted comment-corrupting edit to `cloud/priv/static/app.css`. Cut every branch from
   `origin/main` explicitly; diff files against `git show origin/main:<path>`, never a working tree.
   Do not clean, stash, or reset that checkout — it holds other sessions' work.

9. **The GUI-remake GR set binds this epic — stated here because it was never stated.** Added wave 16
   (D191). `GR11`/`GR25`/`GR116` are defined only in `bp-cloud-gui-remake-charter.md` (`:25`, `:40`,
   `:502`); this charter's preamble inherits ROWS, not RULES, so every GR citation in D153/D157/D165
   and in `app.css:2196`/`:2270` was formally unanchored. It is anchored now. GR25 governs the scope:
   the quarantine forbids EDITING the four pill-family rule blocks (`status-pill`, `dep-pill`,
   `tlv-badge`, `badge`/`fresh-badge`), never CONSUMING them; wrappers (`.fleet-status`,
   `.fleet-main`, `.rail-row`) are outside it. Any slice that must edit inside the fence needs its own
   D157-shaped carve-out ruling, argued as an ADD rather than a restyle.


## Surface fence

**In fence:** `cloud/`, `api/lib/barkpark_web/live/`.

**Standing dispensations** (granted wave 1, narrow, do not widen):
- `api/lib/barkpark/content/mutations.ex` + its tests — the ledger close-bypass guard. Proven
  two-clause, one-file; `api/lib/barkpark/tasks/` never calls `apply_mutations`, so the sanctioned
  CAS path is structurally immune.
- `design/emit.mjs`, `design/check.mjs` — the emit-marker fence. A correct fix cannot live inside
  `cloud/`; 15 of the 18 emitted artifacts are outside both fenced paths.
  **Wave-6 widening (exactly two files, D75):** `design/exemptions.json` and
  `design/emit-manifest.json`. Any tokenization of an app.css hand-stamp SHRINKS the literal ledger
  and reds `check.mjs` Part E unless the baseline moves in the same diff; the manifest is a
  `--write` by-product. Do not widen further.
- `.github/workflows/console-harness.yml` — CI wiring for console instruments.

**Wave-11 dispensation — RECONCILING THE FENCE WITH THREE WAVES OF PRACTICE (D126).** Waves 9 and 10
merged edits to `.github/workflows/cloud.yml`, `.github/workflows/security.yml`,
`.github/required-checks.json` and `scripts/required-checks-*.sh` — all of them outside the fence as
written above. The fence text was stale, not the work. Stated out loud now, narrow, and not to be
widened: `.github/workflows/cloud.yml`, `.github/workflows/security.yml`,
`.github/workflows/required-checks-drift.yml`, `.github/required-checks.json`,
`scripts/required-checks-{generate,apply,floor,verify}.sh`, `scripts/required-checks.test.sh`,
`scripts/registration-*.sh`, `scripts/{cloud,console}-path-escape-check.sh`, and `cloud/Dockerfile`
+ `cloud/.gitignore` (the console's own build/ship path). The PROTECTION PUT itself is an operator
act with an admin token, performed only from a worktree cut from `origin/main` at flip time — never
from the primary checkout, never from CI (no workflow can grant the `administration` scope).

**Wave-2 dispensations** (granted at wave-2 Decide, narrow, stated out loud):
- `docs/ops/merge-gates.md` + `api/.sobelow-skips` — the gate ledger's own honesty. Both are
  claim/reality divergences in the instruments this epic merges through: merge-gates.md never
  classifies Vercel, and the Sobelow baseline pins a finding to `router.ex:2505` that now sits at
  `:2530`, so a stale entry reds `main` itself. Dead centre of the frame; nothing in `cloud/` can fix
  either.

**Out of fence, hands off:** `tooling/grip/` (truth-grip **wave 5** running, PR #5314), `scripts/pds-*`
and `api/lib/barkpark/tenancy/` (PDS **wave 15** running — paper `pds-wave-15-2026-07-21`; wave 2's
survey wrongly reported no wave 15 exists, it does).

## The dark crown

`PLATFORM_ADMIN_EMAILS` is unset on prod. It lives in a gitignored `.env`; zero deploy scripts
reference it; **no commit can set it**. Every authenticated user gets 403 on the operator console.
This is a HUMAN GATE belonging to the repo owner. Note it where relevant; never build around it,
fake it, or file work that depends on it being lit. Same disposition for the QR live-scan proof.

## Decisions

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | Keep the six-band census; re-verify rather than re-derive it | A prior Fable wrote it with full seal context; re-deriving burns the whole budget reproducing a worse version. Re-verification is the cheap part and the only part genuinely missing. |
| D2 | Refetch storm is fixed by **scope-narrowing**, never debounce | Repeats are evenly spaced (1 cold boot + 7 ticks = 40, reproduced from code alone), so a time window suppresses nothing. Four registered types funnel into one 5-endpoint `loadOverview()`. |
| D3 | The narrowing must also cover the `barkpark.*` **prefix fallback** | `app.js:12076` routes ANY unregistered `barkpark.*` type into the same monolith; a synthetic `barkpark.unknown_skew` fired the full 5. Enumerating the four known types leaves the skew path storming. |
| D4 | The refetch protective test fixture must carry **≥1 instance** | An empty fleet short-circuits `loadOverview()` to 2 requests; an empty-fleet fixture would assert "2" and pass forever while the storm rages for every real user. Vacuous green by construction. |
| D5 | Peer-IP: **PIN** the gateway, do not widen to `172.16/12` | Measured: widening let peer `{172,18,0,77}` forge `203.0.113.5`. `cloud-postfix-1` sits on `172.18.0.2` publishing `0.0.0.0:587` — widening hands an internet-facing SMTP container the ability to forge any client's IP and rate-limit bucket. |
| D6 | The pin ships with a compose `networks:` subnet block **in the same PR** | `docker-compose.yml` has no `networks:` block at all; the subnet is allocation-order-dependent (it landed on `.18` only because the default bridge took `.17`). A bare hardcoded tuple rots silently into a no-op with a test asserting it works. |
| D7 | Do **not** touch RemoteIp's `proxies:` list | Measured: widening `loopback_peer?/1` alone is sufficient. RemoteIp resolves from the header chain and never consults `conn.remote_ip`; a builder reasoning from peer-as-last-hop makes a wrong second edit. |
| D8 | SSE ticket: **strict single-use + a client reconnect rewrite** (option b) | Measured in headless Chrome: ANY 401 is terminal (`readyState 2`, browser never retries again). Short-TTL multi-connect shares the identical cliff on TTL expiry, so the client change is mandatory under BOTH designs — and (b) is strictly stronger security for the same client work. |
| D9 | The SSE slice must add a **fourth liveness-chip state** | The chip documents exactly three states and shows `reconnecting` whenever `evtErrored`, which after a 401 is permanently false. Fixing the leak without it trades a security defect for an honesty defect — inside an epic whose frame is "the console stops lying". |
| D10 | Remint on `readyState 0`, never rely on native retry | Under strict single-use the browser's own retry is a guaranteed 401: ~3.0s of dead stream per flap plus one 401 per flap in the access log (noise that reads like credential stuffing). |
| D11 | HEAD fix is a **router-level deny-list plug placed BEFORE `plug(Plug.Head)`** | `Plug.Head.call/2` is `%{conn \| method: "GET"}` and stashes nothing — by the time control reaches the route, HEAD-ness is destroyed, so per-route short-circuits are impossible, not merely inferior. One greppable list is mutation-testable; a scatter of route-local guards is not. |
| D12 | Answer **405 with `allow: GET`**, not 404 | 404 is the lie that `plug(Plug.Head)` (#4481) was landed to remove. "This path exists, that method is refused" is the honest answer. |
| D13 | Deny-list, not opt-in route tagging | Opt-in requires auditing all 56 GETs now plus every future one, and inverts the default so a forgotten new route silently 404s again. Deny-list fails open on a NEW mutating GET — the milder failure. |
| D14 | Match on `conn.path_info` segment lists, never a string prefix | The initiator and callback share a prefix and `path_params` are unpopulated at plug time (this runs before `:match`); a `starts_with?` guard is either over- or under-broad. |
| D15 | The HEAD slice also **prunes `oauth_states`** | Both GET legs `Repo.insert!` a row per unauthenticated hit and the ONLY delete anywhere is the single-row `consume_state`. No reaper references `OAuth.State`; the Oban crontab schedules none. Unauthenticated unbounded table growth. |
| D16 | Frame slice D as a **response-header leak**, not CWE-598 | The token rides the Location *fragment*, so it does not propagate via Referer or the next hop's access log. Writing CWE-598 into the brief makes a builder fix the wrong thing. |
| D17 | cssom-parity wires as a **separate Node-22 job**, not a fourth step | `console-unit` pins `node-version: 20`; the instrument needs global `WebSocket` (Node 22+). Bumping the whole job would silently change the runtime under two currently-working gates to buy nothing. |
| D18 | No `setup-chrome` action; set `CHROME=/usr/bin/google-chrome` explicitly | Proven on a real runner (PR #5242, run 29801714262): ubuntu-latest ships Chrome 150 at all four candidate paths, parity PASS in 5.9s. `findChrome()` honours `CHROME` but NOT the runner's own `CHROME_BIN`. Pinning turns a future missing binary into an honest exit-2 guard instead of a silent fallthrough. |
| D19 | The wiring must make an env failure exit **2 (guard)**, not 1 (miss) | Under Node 20 the instrument prints `!! PARITY ERROR: WebSocket is not defined` and exits **1** — a misconfigured runner reds the PR with a message that reads like a stylesheet defect. Self-defeating for this epic. |
| D20 | Assert a minimum authored-rule-head **count floor** | Mutation-proving found a blind spot: INSERTING a stray `/*` passes with MISSES 0 (both sides swallow symmetrically, 1201→1188). The gate is proven able to fail only for the de-opened direction. One line closes the hole. |
| D21 | Fix the emit-marker fence in **`emit.mjs`**, not via `check.mjs`-vs-origin/main | `evaluate()` already computes `current` and `expected` in the same pass and knows `drift` before `writeFileSync` — zero new I/O. `check.mjs` compares `build()` to itself after a write (tautological PASS) and a git-diff variant is after-the-fact DETECTION of an already-destroyed file, plus a new external dep in a repo that runs out of worktrees. |
| D22 | Guard the ledger bypass at **both** patch clauses, keyed on `source:` | The compound clause (`:266`) is exploitable via its OWN `set` merge, independently of the plain clause (`:298`); guarding one leaves the other fully open. `source: :sync`/`:api` is already threaded AND read with a safe default — no new plumbing. |
| D23 | Do not reopen `gr-blk-shootsh-scen-suggester` | Its successor `gr-bl-shootsh-scen-suggester-false-done` is already open under this epic. Blind reopening creates a duplicate pair for one fix. Land under the successor; reference the original when disposing of it. |
| D24 | Do not re-parent `gr-blk-studio-presence-perf-flake` | L1-verified: its `parent_id` is already `task-96a908af98698118` ("Felix pristine initiative"), exactly where the epic's own criterion 1 sends it. Stamp the evidence; leave it. |
| D25 | **Wave 1's "AFTER X merges" serialization is RETIRED.** HEAD and SSE build in parallel from `origin/main` | It was file-truth (three slices touch one 10k-line router) mistaken for semantic dependency. Proven by an actual merge, not line arithmetic: all four stranded branches merge into `origin/main` with **exit 0, zero CONFLICT lines, and a byte-identical tree in reverse order**. The whole peer-ip change is ONE hunk `@@ -349,34 +349,81 @@`, so `plug(Plug.Head)` stays at **284 unchanged** and everything below shifts exactly **+47**. The ordering cost is paid once, at merge, by the lead. |
| D26 | The joint-defect fence is **"the mint PATH has no GET route"**, NOT "the mint is a POST" | The lead's stated mechanism is wrong and the wrong version does not protect. `Plug.Head` rewrites HEAD→GET *unconditionally, before matching*; it has no notion of POST. Counterexample measured: `HEAD /v1/tokens` returns **401, not 404**, because `get "/v1/tokens"` exists beside the POST — and authenticated, it returns **200 with 0 PATs created**, having reached the *list* handler. POST-only 404s only because no GET clause matches. A future `get "/v1/auth/sse-ticket"` would silently reopen the trap while every route was still a POST. |
| D27 | SSE mint route: **`post "/v1/auth/sse-ticket"` at `router.ex:1283`** | Between `delete "/v1/auth/logout"` (ends :1282) and the `# GET /v1/account/sessions` comment (:1284) — the authenticated session-lifecycle cluster, authenticated by the session bearer exactly as logout is, ~137 lines above its consumer `get "/v1/events"` (:1420). `POST /v1/tokens` (:3921) is the exact sibling shape. No `get` exists at that path, so D26 holds. |
| D28 | **The consume is MATCHED-ROW-ONLY. Do not copy the prescribed template.** | The wave-1 brief orders the builder to copy `reset_password_by_token`'s consume. Measured: it calls `revoke_reset_tokens(uid, now)` whose WHERE is `user_id + context + is_nil(revoked_at)` — **no `token_hash`** — so consuming one token revokes every live sibling (proven: 2 live → consume 1 → other stamped, count 2→0). The reset *mint* supersedes too. Under D10's remint that is a **two-tab mutual-eviction storm**: tab B mints → A's ticket dies → A 401s → A remints → B dies → forever, one 401 per iteration, the exact log noise D10 exists to prevent. Sibling-wide is right for `reset` (a credential-reset *event*, one link at a time); an SSE ticket is a *per-connection* artifact and the user legitimately holds one per tab. |
| D29 | Take **mint from `2fa_pending`, verify from `verify_user_session_token`, consume from NEITHER** | All three prescribed templates were measured. `create_two_factor_pending_token` is matched-row-only (2 mints → 2 live, both verify) — safe. `delete_two_factor_pending_tokens` is `delete_all` by user_id — sibling-wide. And `verify_two_factor_pending_token` **does not filter `revoked_at`** (proven: a token stamped `revoked_at` still verifies), so a builder copying it ships a ticket whose single-use guarantee is unenforceable. `verify_user_session_token` (:548-568) filters context + `revoked_at` + `expires_at` and is the correct verify. |
| D30 | TTL = **60 seconds**, as `@sse_ticket_validity_seconds 60` (seconds, not the house `*_minutes`) | The live window is only mint→connect: an XHR POST returns it and `new EventSource(...)` opens within one RTT. 20× Chrome's measured ~3000ms retry, satisfying criterion 7's "do not tune tightly against 3s". Shortest-lived credential in the codebase (`@two_factor_pending_minutes 5`, `@state_ttl_seconds 600`, reset 60min, sessions 30d) — correct for a single-use credential consumed in one RTT. Expiry is **self-healing** under D10 (one extra mint round-trip, never a dead stream), so "too short" is bounded and invisible and there is no reason to buy margin. Minutes-granularity invites "round up to 5" and a silent 5× exposure. |
| D31 | **`cloud/priv/static/app.css` joins the SSE slice's fence** | D9's fourth state does not render "unstyled" — it renders **grey**, which is worse, because grey is plausible. Measured in headless Chrome against `origin/main`: a fourth `data-state` matches **0 author rules** and falls back to `.live-dot { background: var(--dim) }` = `rgb(95,106,123)` — a static neutral dot, **visually calmer than the less-severe `reconnecting`** (amber + `live-breathe` animation + amber chip). The most severe state would paint the calmest. `--danger`/`--danger-soft` exist in both light (:69-70) and dark (:106-107) token blocks, so the rule is append-only with zero token edits. |
| D32 | The decisive argument for D31 is the **gate gap**, not the pixel | No gate catches a missing attribute-state rule. `__css_check` E2 extracts only class emissions (`/\.className\s*=\s*"…"/`, `/classList\.(?:add\|remove\|toggle)\(…/`); the chip sets state via `chip.setAttribute("data-state", state)` (app.js:11871), invisible to both regexes. `cssom-parity.mjs` contains **zero** `data-state` references — it proves authored rules reach the browser, never that emitted states have rules. A builder shipping the fourth state without app.css gets a **fully green board on a dead-looking state**: a false green on the fix for a false green. |
| D33 | The SSE slice must widen `__bpTestHook` and add a **closed-enum** test | Measured vacuous-green: `liveDotState`'s 4 existing tests are pure 3-arg equality checks and **no test asserts the return is a closed set**, so JS's permissive arity keeps them all green after a 4th state lands — `node --test` would report green while asserting zero new behaviour. Worse, the DOM assertions are *unwritable today*: `renderLivenessChip()` takes zero params and reads `evtErrored`/`lastEventMs` from private closure state with no setter, and `LIVE_CHIP_COPY`/`LIVE_CHIP_ARIA` are not exported at all. Also: an unknown state falls through `LIVE_CHIP_COPY[state] \|\| "Live"` (:11874) and `LIVE_CHIP_ARIA[state] \|\| "Live updates"` (:11881) — the chip **announcing "Live" over a permanently dead stream**. |
| D34 | The HEAD deny-list **does not grow**, and the floor is **45, not ~36** | Measured over the real `Plug.Head` pipeline: every authenticated GET writes `user_tokens.last_used_at` — **unthrottled** on the session path (`touch_last_used/2` is a bare `update_all`, two HEADs 11ms apart both wrote), throttled to 60s on the PAT path. Census: 56 GETs, 50 authenticated, 5 agent/worker → **45 writers**. The `~36` figure could not be reproduced by any method and is **retired**, not reconciled; the undercount came from 9 routes authenticating only via local wrappers (`with_team_role`, `with_team_site`, `require_user_sse`, `proxy_instance_webhook`) that a grep for `Auth.require_` cannot see. Denying HEAD across 80% of the API reinstates the 404 lie #4481 removed. |
| D35 | The write is not even gated on **authorization** — say so in the evidence | Unclaimed by anyone before wave 2: `HEAD /v1/operator/fleet` with a valid session and a non-operator email answers **403 and still moved `last_used_at`**, because `require_platform_operator/2` calls `require_user/2` first. The touch is a property of *authentication*, not of any route, so there is no subset to carve out. This sharpens D34 rather than weakening it. |
| D36 | Ledger guard extends to `createOrReplace` + `replace` only; **`create`/`createIfNotExists` are EXEMPT by structure** | Measured on all four: `createOrReplace` and `replace` close an existing claimed open task unfenced; `create` returns `{:error, :conflict}` and `createIfNotExists` a silent `noop`. The exemption is structural, not preference: **there is no prior revision on a fresh create**, so D22's `ifRevisionID` escape hatch is undefined and the same guard shape degrades from *fence* to *unconditional ban* — breaking importers the substrate explicitly anticipates (migration `20260528100000`). `existing == nil -> :ok` expresses the whole fork. |
| D37 | These two doors **erase the claimant**, and `ifRevisionID` does not save it — so the guard must also refuse a claim-drop | Strictly larger blast radius than the patch door D22 fences. The patch clause computes `merged = existing.content \|> Map.merge(set)`, so `claim` **survives**; `createOrReplace`/`replace` write `attrs["content"]` **wholesale**, so `after = {"done", nil}` — the claim is *gone*, not stale. Measured: with a **correct** `ifRevisionID` the result is still `lifecycle="done" claim=nil`. Rev is a concurrency fence, never an attribution one. The row is then permanently uncloseable through the sanctioned path (`Tasks.Close` rejects an already-terminal row as `stale_claim`). |
| D38 | The click oracle is **BUILT**, and its scope is the enumerated **8 unfixtured DELETEs**, not the charter's "six" | Prototyped and run: +184/−10 across two files, all 86 pre-existing scenarios still green, and the decisive mutation — changing the per-row Revoke from `addEventListener("click")` to `("mousedown")`, a genuinely dead button with **every source string preserved** — is caught by the oracle (exit 1) and **missed by the 9,074-line `__app.test.mjs` (exit 0)**. That is the capability gap: existing guards assert source strings, this one asserts behaviour. | D155 | **THE SCRUB LANDS AS `FailureCopy.scrub/1` WRAPPING THE WHOLE COND, THE SCOPE IS SIX CHANNELS NOT FOUR, AND THE PREVIEW HARNESS IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO IT** | Placement proven three ways by mutation. (a) POST-cond, never pre-cond: `reason |> scrub() |> classify()` SHIFTS classification — `"client_secret=timeout"` renders `"[redacted]"` pre-scrub and `"A network step timed out."` post-scrub. Post-scrub also makes "does not alter matched-class output" free and provable, because every class arm returns a LITERAL. (b) The scrub WRAPS the cond (`humanize(reason) = reason |> classify() |> scrub()`), never sits inside the `typed_refusal?` arm — mutated and red: moving it inside makes it a NO-OP for exactly the string that matters (`"ssh: remote said Authorization: Bearer sk-live-…"` reaches the person verbatim). (c) No laundering: a typed refusal carrying a secret comes out still carrying `E_PATH_TRAVERSAL` and its entry, with `[redacted]` where the token was. **SIX CHANNELS, NOT FOUR:** `deployment_json` emits `console: d.console || []` (router.ex:10131) and `detail: d.detail` (:10134) RAW three lines below the humanized `failure_reason` (:10108), and `Sites.Deploy.fail/2` (deploy.ex:912-917) writes `failure_reason: reason, detail: reason` — **the same string** — so a four-channel scope ships a redacted field next to its unredacted twin, in the same payload, under a comment saying that must never happen. **THE PATTERN SET IS THE WHOLE RISK: a generic `\b[A-Za-z0-9]{40,}\b` REDACTS A 40-CHAR GIT SHA** ("build of 0f28d541e… failed" → "build of [redacted] failed") — a real deploy-rail copy shift, and the full 2600-test suite passed under it, so suite green does NOT cover the patterns. **AND THE INHERITED MUST-RUN IS REFUTED: `smoke.mjs:1272` does NOT red under the scrub and CANNOT — it boots app.js in a node:vm sandbox over `scenarios.mjs:2172`, with no Elixir in the loop; it ran 99/99 green with the full scrub live.** This slice's proof standard is ExUnit + driven HTTP; citing smoke.mjs green is vacuous. DESIGN.md §5's permission for raw provider strings "only in the timeline's fail/console fold" is untouched — the scrub redacts SECRET-SHAPED substrings, not provider jargon. |

A REAL toggle is the only shape with headroom (contentTop 56, fraction 0.132, content reach 4/17 → 8/17 with no interaction) but it costs +3 rule heads, THREE new measured Q2 defects (its `Menu` button squeezes `select#bp-theme-picker` at 390 — `scrollWidth 72 > clientWidth 61`, "Evergreen" cut with no cue, on three cells), a 32px padding ghost under `max-height: 0`, and a tab-order hole (all 15 stops stay focusable inside a 32px slit). **That is a wave of its own, not a slice.** **THE BOUNDED CLAIM**: no fraction and no shell shape delivers the person test on the worst screen — on `billing-trial` **0 of 2 controls inside `.content` are reachable at every width ≤720, at 390/667/800, in both themes, under EVERY candidate including full collapse**, because the CONTENT puts the first CTA 542.5px below `.content`'s top invariantly. A shell slice removes the shell's 272px and NOTHING MORE; the unreachable CTA is D178's grid defect and the two must not be conflated in a criterion. |

The 34vh cap flattened a 466/503/679 spread of natural nav heights to one number, so a uniform shell has no worst screen and the row closes BY CONTENT. **contentTop does not grow below 619 — it does not move AT ALL**, because at ≤720 the nav is a full-width vertical stack whose `scrollHeight` is byte-identical at 320 and 720 (503 operator / 466 overview-fleet / 679 tokens-member) and the cap always binds. Measured at 320/360/375/390/500/719/721: `.cli-chip-code` reports `scrollWidth 337 == clientWidth 337`, `codeTrunc:false`, full text `bp cloud instance resurrect shop --provider hetzner`. **Nothing is cut today.** The defect is a RIGID PARENT: `.archive-resurrect { flex: 0 0 auto }` (`app.css:3850`, NOT the `:3797` the direction cited — anchor by content) lays a 460.2px cluster inside a 286px row box. **AND 721 IS THE MILDEST INSTANCE, NOT THE DEFECT**: page overflow is **171px @320**, 131 @360, 116 @375, 101 @390, 0 at 500-719, and 10 at 721 — two disjoint bands (320–~495 and 721+), of which the sweep can only ever see the second because `boundaryWalk`'s floor is 619. **IT IS ALSO FIXTURE-BOUND BY 2.2x**: the command is `"bp cloud instance resurrect " <> slug <> " --provider " <> kind` (`app.js:1525`) and the registry caps a slug at 63 (`registry/barkpark.ex:468`), so the product maximum is 110 chars → 726px of chip; driven with a 63-char slug on UNMODIFIED CSS the overflow is **554 @320 and 393 @721** and the band reaches past 1133px. **THE TRAP IS REAL AND WAS COMPLETED TO PROVE IT**: `app.css:3266` already ships `overflow:hidden; text-overflow:ellipsis` on `.cli-chip-code`, so the whole "shrink the chip" fix is `min-width:0` on two flex items — driven, it takes page overflow to 0 at 320/375/721 (**Q1 SIDEWAYS GOES GREEN**) while cutting 35% of the command at 320 and **70% at a max-length slug**, with the cut landing inside the slug, the one variable the operator needs. A green sweep over a cut shell command is precisely the failure this epic exists to prevent. **RULING: wrap + a BOUNDED SCROLLER** — `flex-basis:100%; flex-wrap:wrap; min-width:0` on `.archive-resurrect` plus `min-width:0; max-width:100%; overflow-x:auto` on its chip, scoped to `.archive-resurrect .cli-chip` (`.cli-chip` is SHARED with the webhook chips). Driven at 320/375/721/1280 x {light,dark} x {667,800} with a 63-char product-maximum slug: **16/16 cells at page overflow 0**, and the remaining command is REACHABLE rather than destroyed. The one-rule `flex-basis:100%` alone FAILS at 320/375/390 (86/31/16px residual) — it fixes only 500 and 721. The scroll cue must be TWO-STATE (present only while `scrollWidth > clientWidth`) or it trips the sweep's own `CUE_STUCK` at ≥721 where the chip fits whole. |
| D178 | **THE BILLING TIER CTA IS NOT A BOUNDARY DEFECT — IT IS UNBOUNDED, AND THE FIX IS `auto-fit` WITH THE 900 RULE KEPT** | `cch-w14-bl-billing-tier-cta-901` calls it "a boundary defect, invisible one pixel lower". Driven at 901/1200/1300/1400/1440/1500/1520/1600/1700 it reds at **every one**, and from 1300 up the numbers FREEZE at `scrollWidth 147 > clientWidth 136` — because `.content { max-width: 1040px }` (`app.css:906`) stops the grid growing at 992px of track, so five `minmax(0,1fr)` columns are permanently 188.8px and the CTA is permanently 11px short. **A 4K monitor shows the same cut label as a 901px window.** (The row's cited `app.css:1045` is also off by one; the rule is at `:1044`.) The mechanism is two faults in one declaration: `minmax(0,1fr)` lets a track shrink below its content without limit, AND `repeat(5,…)` is hardcoded while `billing-trial` renders three tiers, so at 901 the two EMPTY tracks eat 236px of a 579px grid. **RULING: `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(230px, 1fr))`, KEEPING the `@media (max-width: 900px)` rule.** Measured clean at 619/899/900/901/1040/1200/1700 and — critically — **it survives a FIVE-TIER scenario with zero new clips on any tier's button** (992px track admits 4 tracks at 242, five cards wrap 4+1 at 239 each, ~50px margin per track). Only the sidecar unit test catches it. |
| D179 | **THE UNBOUNDED LEAD COLUMN IS A FLEX-BASIS DEFECT, NOT A `max-width` ONE — THE REMEDY IS TWO DECLARATIONS AND MUST BE SCOPED TO `min-width: 900px`** | The row's own root cause is half wrong. At the 900 control the WORST cell is `fleet-v4` row 1 — `.fleet-url` 60/144 (58% of the hostname unrendered) — whose `.fleet-status` is **131.80px, the NARROWEST pill on the screen**, while its `.fleet-badges` is 331.80px. Mechanism: `.fleet-main { min-width: 0; flex: 1 }` is `flex: 1 1 0%` — a ZERO hypothetical size, so the flex line never registers as overflowing, `.fleet-badges` (`flex: 0 1 auto`) is never asked to shrink, and the hostname is the structural loser regardless of the pill. It is also SPILL, not truncation: `.fleet-url` computes `overflow: visible`, so the hostname is painted THROUGH the badge chips (url box ends 482.75, content reaches 566.80, badges begin 498.75). **RULING: `@media (min-width: 900px) { .fleet-row { flex-wrap: wrap } .fleet-main { flex: 1 1 auto; min-width: 200px } }`** — driven over 769/830/899/900/940/983/1000/1100/1200 x both themes on mixed-fleet + fleet-v4: clip cells **12 → 0**, ellipsed money messages 0 → 0, page overflow 0 → 0, `minMainW` 59.95 → 202.89, spill overlaps 6 → 0, every badge box at its exact baseline width, and the ≤899 stacked band plus the 1200 desktop control **byte-identical to baseline**. A builder who "simplifies away" the media block ships an 846px sideways scroll on a scenario no gate renders. Three shapes are REFUTED by measurement: `max-width: 45%` ellipses the money message in **60 of 64 cells** including the stacked band (39% of "Payment failed — subscription past due" at 769); `overflow-wrap: anywhere` scores 0 clips while growing `.fleet-url` 36→90px; and `.fleet-main{flex:1 1 auto} + .fleet-badges{min-width:0}` scores 0 on every text metric while collapsing `.fleet-badges` to **29.83px around an unshrinkable 66.33px chip** — the mirror image of the shred, invisible to a clip-only scorer. **AND THE GUARD IS BLIND TO ITS OWN SUBJECT**: `overflow-guard.mjs`'s W13 leg asserts only `documentElement.scrollWidth` and prints "108/108 clean" on the very tree where 8 cells clip at its own 900 control — AND no workflow runs it. A C slice must add an ELEMENT-level assertion; leaning on the page-level one certifies the regression. **The row's headline 769px numbers (348.02 on a 489px row) are HISTORY** — at 769 today the row is stacked and every column measures 451px; amend by measurement before a builder claims it. |
| D180 | **THE WIDENING: THEME IS A FRESH LOAD AND IS COVERAGE NOT YIELD, HEIGHT NEEDS A DECLARED SET BECAUSE NO ARTIFACT DECLARES ONE, AND THE SCENARIO ALLOWLIST IS NAME-KEYED WITH FAMILY REASONS AND A FATAL STALENESS CLAUSE** | **THEME.** `app.css` declares exactly 2 modes (`[data-theme="dark"]` plus the `:root` default, the light member coming from `index.html:2`, NOT from the stylesheet) and **ZERO `prefers-color-scheme` rules** — so a "derive from `prefers-color-scheme`" plan derives an EMPTY set. All 8 emitting dark rules use only `background`/`color`/`border-color`; the `--elev-*` deltas are consumed by six `box-shadow` sites and nothing else. **The CSS structurally cannot produce a theme-dependent geometry defect** — say that honestly rather than promising finds. Confirmed by driving: light, dark-load and dark-flip give byte-identical Q1/Q2/Q3 on all 78 sampled cells. The ONE named yield class is CONTENT: `themeLabelText()` (`app.js:3530`) makes `#theme-label` read "Light" in dark and "Dark" in light — the only theme-differentiated measurement ever recorded in this epic (`#theme-toggle` right 778/780, 336-load audit, changed no verdict). **THEME MUST BE A FRESH `?theme=` LOAD, NEVER A RUNTIME ATTRIBUTE FLIP**: a flip leaves the console lying about itself (`{attr:"dark", label:"Dark", aria:"Switch to dark theme", ls:"light"}` vs a loaded `{attr:"dark", label:"Light", …, ls:"dark"}`) — it would measure the wrong control string and could not see a `themeLabelText` defect at all, and this does NOT settle out at 800ms. At the naive 32ms settle it is also mid-transition (8 distinct background colours incl. intermediate greys); settling it honestly costs 0.95 s/cell and erases its own speed advantage. **HEIGHT.** There is NO artifact to derive from — `app.css` has **zero** height-bearing `@media`, and the 16 `vh`-bearing declarations are continuous rules, not boundaries. Ship BOTH halves: a derived `parseHeightBreakpoints` twin that refuses on any height-bearing `@media` the set does not straddle (vacuously green today, mutation-proven to exit 2 on `@media (max-height: 600px)` — and note the shipped sweep PARSES that rule, COUNTS it as a 21st prelude, and SILENTLY DISCARDS it because `parseWidthClause` gates on `\bwidth\b`), AND a declared `HEIGHTS` set with a written reason per value and a shrink refusal mirroring `BREAKPOINTS`. **`HEIGHTS` MUST INCLUDE A LANDSCAPE VALUE (390)** — it is the binding height for D174's bar and the repo currently carries three disagreeing committed heights (`breakpoint-sweep` 800, `modal-oracle` 900, S2's landscape 667/390). **SCENARIO.** 26 cells cover **25 DISTINCT scenarios of 99** (`mixed-fleet` is used twice — "26 of 99" over-counts by one, though the 74-residue figure is right). The unit is **UNIT C: 74 NAME-KEYED entries pointing at 13 ARTIFACT-DERIVED family reasons** (`familyOf` = `pathname≠"/"` → `path:<pathname>`, else `deepLink.split("/")[0]` → `hash:<head>`, else `no-deeplink`; the naive name-prefix split gives 27 families). Adjudicated by mutation, not preference: a 13-entry FAMILY-keyed list **fails 3 of 4 mutations** — it swallows a 100th scenario with no deepLink, swallows one inside the existing 20-member `hash:#instance` family, and goes green while its entry rots when a multi-member-family scenario gains a cell. UNIT A (74 filed rows) is mechanically IDENTICAL to UNIT C on all four mutations and buys ZERO refusal power for 74 permanently-open rows — the residue already has a filed owner (`gr-blk-accent-scenario-sweep`). **THE ALLOWLIST MUST SHIP AS A COMMITTED LITERAL**: an allowlist computed from the current residue reports all three units green even UNDER mutation, because it grows with the artifact and can never refuse anything — it looks itemised, it is even "artifact-derived", and it is 100% vacuous. **AND THE STALENESS CLAUSE MUST BE FATAL**: `__css_check`'s allowlist staleness reporter is `console.log`, never `errors.push` — a bogus `ALLOW_RAW_COLORS` entry matching no line prints `stale … prune it` and **exits 0**. That is the one thing not to copy. |
| D181 | **NO TASK VERB CAN WRITE A CRITERION'S TEXT — AMENDMENT IS A WHOLE-ARRAY DOC PATCH, AND IT WILL 409 THE HOLDER'S CLOSE** | `bp task stamp` and `bp task close --set criteria:=[…]` funnel through ONE merger, `Tasks.Internal.merge_criteria/2`; the `"criterion"` key is read ONLY as a CAS guard (`internal.ex:219-225`, mismatch → `:criteria_mismatch`, whole write aborted) and `apply_entry_update/2` touches exactly `met`, `evidence`, `attempts`. **There is no code path in the task API that writes `acceptance_criteria[i].criterion`.** The sole path is a plain document write — `bp doc patch task <id> --set 'acceptance_criteria:=[…]'` (or `/v1/data/mutate`) — whose gate (`plugins/tasks.ex:230-258`) validates SHAPE only, with no holder/epoch/verb-redirect check. It writes a DRAFT: a `bp doc publish` is required because gates and boards read the published ledger. **CONSEQUENCE THAT MUST BE SEQUENCED**: the claim carries `work_field_digests.acceptance_criteria`, so amending the array 409s the holder's later `bp task close` with `doc_changed_since_claim` — the close must carry `--set observed_rev=<current_rev>`. **THE TWO AMENDMENTS, AND BOTH INDEX CITATIONS IN THE WISH ARE OFF BY ONE** — amend by TEXT, never by index. `cch-w14-s3`'s honest miss is the 2x-height bar (0-based index 2); `cch-w14-s1`'s is the `--render`/745.88 row (0-based index 6). **AND A THIRD AMENDMENT IS NOW STRUCTURALLY OWED**: `cch-w14-bl-independent-review-owed` criterion 4 ("recorded BEFORE it merges") is UNSATISFIABLE — #8659 merged 2026-07-31T21:43:45Z with `"reviews":[]` and `"comments":[]`, zero of each, and no past merge becomes pre-reviewed. Precedent (PDS wave 30, PDS-D422, paid by PR #8648): an amendment is legitimate only if it is PAID by measurement and RECORDS the measurement, not the opinion; and a sweep that finds another slice's criterion unsatisfiable FILES a row rather than rewriting it silently. |

The cost is INFORMATION LOSS, not misinformation — but it is bigger than the PR body says, because it swallows LIVE previews too. **THE LABEL RULING**: keep the visible pill at "Not deployed" and qualify the ACCESSIBLE NAME/`title` to "Not deployed to production" — the row already renders `on <a class="site-inst-link">Production</a>` (the INSTANCE name) inches away, so the visible-text form sets "production" twice meaning two different things, and the label a preview-only site actually wants ("Preview only") requires the very 5th key the keyset law forbids. **THE UNFILED DEFECT, FOUND ONLY BY DRIVING**: `siteOpenLink(siteLiveUrl(...))` (`app.js:7285`, called at `:7534` and `:9497`) is gated ONLY on a URL string existing, NEVER on deployment state — the acme-labs row paints `Not deployed` and `Visit ↗ title="Open the live site"` side by side, and #8659 WIDENED the population this hits. **CENSUS HONESTY**: no production DB is reachable, and the design corpus is damning for a different reason — **44 site rows, 40 (91%) with `last_deployment == null`, and ZERO modelling a preview-only site.** The ruling's entire cost is unmodelled in the corpus every instrument in this epic drives. |

## Roadmap

### Wave 19 — the guard loses in CI, and the screens keep getting paid

Wave 18 gave `overflow-guard.mjs` a job after five waves of assertions sat in a file CI never ran.
The row that bought it is **not closed**, and its two open criteria are the whole point: the wiring
must be MUTATION-PROVEN — a deliberate regression turns the CI job red, and reverting turns it green.
The job exists (`console-harness.yml:487`, the 0/1/2 ladder, `Console gate`'s `needs:`). **What is
unproven is that it can LOSE.** A wiring slice that cannot be shown to fail is exactly the disease it
was written to cure.

Wave 19's thesis: **PROVE THE WIRING CAN LOSE, THEN SPEND THE PROOF.** After this wave, "the guard is
wired" stops meaning "a job exists" and starts meaning "we watched TWO CI contexts move together on a
deliberate break and back on its revert, and quoted both run ids." Verification then bought three
things the direction did not have (D217 `Console gate` is NOT merge-blocking, so the proof is
AGGREGATOR PROPAGATION and must say so; D218 the guard can FALSE-GREEN under a narrower font face,
which is the wave's headline finding; D219 the topbar band is 620 and not 430, which retires the
wish's own `BREAKPOINTS` obligation by measurement) and refuted one framing outright (D220 — the wrap
recipe the epic has copied three times DOES NOT FIX the fourth host, so D210's extraction trigger
fires on a host the extraction would misfix, and the answer is an instrument, not a refactor).

The standing test carries forward with wave 18's sharper edge: *a fix whose only guard is a sentence
is not fixed* — **and a guard no job runs is a sentence with a shebang.** Wave 19 adds the third
clause the font probe forced: **and a guard that cannot lose is a sentence with an exit code.**

Ratio: four person-facing slices, six person-hit defects (D227), none invented, and the operator pill
is EXCLUDED from the count by L1 evidence (D221) rather than argued into it. The baseline one-hand law
(D202) is the wave's only real sequencer — four slices want `cssom-heads.baseline`, so they run as a
chain (`s2 → s6 → s8 → s7`) and only `s2` builds this run.

### Wave 18 — the front screen, and the guard that proves it stays fixed

The most-seen surface in this product hides **66% of why an instance is degraded** — and on the
mixed fleet it hides **81%**. Five waves of person-facing fixes have shipped their proof into
`overflow-guard.mjs`, a 1131-line instrument that **no `run:` step has ever invoked**: the single
mention of it across `.github`, `scripts` and `Makefile` is a comment at `console-harness.yml:272`
whose own stated proof ("`grep -rn overflow-guard .github/` still exits 1") is falsified by that
comment containing the string. Wave 16's standing test was *a fix whose only guard is a sentence is
not fixed*; wave 18's successor is **A GUARD NO JOB RUNS IS A SENTENCE WITH A SHEBANG** — and the
front screen is the right place to prove it, because the most-seen fix in the product is the one that
most deserves to never regress.

**The wave's sharpest self-attack was refuted by running it (D208).** The fear that a
never-executed instrument reds `Console gate` on day one is false: the guard exits 0 on untouched
`origin/main` in 12.6s on a quiet host, `FLEET_KNOWN` is `[]` so the fail-closed allowlist cannot
fire, and deleting a merged remedy makes it lose at exit 1 naming the exact cell. Wiring is a
~20-line copy of an existing job, not a repair — but `console-gate` names its five upstreams BY HAND
and **omitting the `decide()` line is the one silent failure**, so the review instruction is a count
(D209).

Verification changed what a builder builds in seven places. **D205** confirms D197's remedy to the
pixel and names the baseline **1256** — while refuting D197's ratified cost sentence, which quotes
pill heights as card heights and inverts the comparison at 360/390. **D206** finds the 24px pin is
fixture-specific and **has already flipped in production**: the dominant server string is "Health
unknown · Agent offline", three characters longer, under which the defect is 69.9% at 320 and still
10.8% at 430 — so the criteria name the fixture string and assert invariants above 320 rather than
height literals. **D207** finds the same defect is worse on `mixed-fleet` (81% at 320, and red at
430 where the filed fixture is clean), and an emitter census settles `cch-w14`'s criterion 3 honestly:
zero red at >=900 across nine wrappers, 52 of 384 red below it, in exactly the two front-screen hosts.
**D210** takes the third copy of the wrap recipe deliberately and files the extraction against the
fourth host. **D211** pays four residue rows on driven bytes and amends two criteria BY MEASUREMENT
rather than moving a bar. **D213** refutes the wish's own axis fact for the eighth time — five
breakpoints, fifteen widths; "five/13" names no state that ever existed — and finds the row filed to
fix stale width prose says "12 widths" in its own description. **D215** re-derives the E11 inventory
at **19** (it moved from 18 this week) and replaces criterion 3's literal with a recipe.

**Ratio, counted not claimed: FOUR person-facing slices against a floor of four, and TWO
instruments.** Six slices: four in round 1 (the attention pill + its own leg, the guard's CI job, the
residue ledger, the scheme-ful door), two in round 2 (the topbar phone-band wrap after the pill,
because it is the SECOND baseline holder at +3 and D212 refutes the "sole holder" premise; then the
citation gate, whose inventory must be re-derived after every anchor-moving sibling has merged).
Fable unavailable — every slice opus. Paper: `cloud-console-hardening-wave-18-2026-08-01`.

### Wave 17 — put a person back in front of six screens, and let exactly one hand hold the baseline

Wave 16 was honest about its own shortfall — "s4, s6 and s8 ARE the three person-facing slices this
wave planned and did not fly" — and this wave took that sentence as its brief. The ratio, **counted
not claimed: FIVE person-facing slices against a floor of four, and ONE instrument.** The topbar
slice alone pays three filed rows.

Verification changed what a builder builds in six places, and two of them reverse a charter ruling.
**D195** proves the topbar is not zero-sum but **over-subscribed by ~50px at 320** — the four
`.topbar-right` children need 341.58px inside a 292px bar, so no redistributive remedy exists at all;
eleven candidates were driven and the only one that clears every child at full labels in 20/20 cells
is a phone-band WRAP, which fuses the trial chip, the past-due chip and the theme picker into ONE
slice and ratifies a vertical cost (topbar 56 → 116.5px at 320) rather than letting a builder discover
it. **D199** refutes D194 twice: its cited detail resolution (`app.js:9459-9461`) is
UNIMPLEMENTABLE because `last_deployment` never reaches the detail payload, and its "one predicate
cannot serve both surfaces" is false — `current_deployment_id` rides `site_json/2` onto both, and the
corpus only made it look wrong because the fixtures assert a state the server cannot produce.
**D200** finds s8 is SIX artifacts, that its filed before-number is exactly half the truth (56, not
28, since wave 16 doubled the theme axis), and that the obvious re-arm of its own mutation is VACUOUS
— it passes a byte-diff clamp while changing nothing, because 899 is declared three times. **D201**
shows s7's own prescribed regex is a NET LOSS against the gate shipping today, and that its stated
justification is a phantom that a real file (`cssom-heads.baseline`, nine `app.css <bytes> B` records)
rescues. **D196** kills the s6/trial-chip bundle by measurement (30 of 30 phone cells unmoved by every
s6-family shape) and catches a MOVE that PARITY-PASSED at exactly 1253 while regressing six cells that
are clean on main — a net-zero head count is not evidence a move preserved behaviour. **D197/D198**
split the attention pill into two hosts, take the `.instance-card-head` half at +4 rather than a +3
variant that would forfeit the old-length-zero diff proof, and demand a bottom-edge assertion because
the naive remedy scores a perfect 1.000 on every horizontal metric in this repo while painting 24px
outside the capsule.

**D202** builds the baseline queue on measured deltas (s8 −1, attention +4, topbar +3, s6 net 0,
s4 0, s7 0) and places the down-bump first and alone. **D204** records that Leg B cannot see below
619px on any axis, so every phone proof this wave writes is hand-driven and labelled. **D203** pays
both free closes on driven before/after over #8818's merged bytes and refuses to let criterion 3 say
"residue filed" until the `.op-gate` row exists — so this wave files it.

Six slices: three in round 1 (s4, s8, s6 — file-disjoint or region-disjoint with s8 the sole baseline
holder), two in round 2 (s7 and the attention pill, both after s8 merges), one in round 3 (the topbar
wrap, after s8 and the attention pill, because its own page-scroll criterion is unsatisfiable until s8
lands). Paper: `cloud-console-hardening-wave-17-2026-08-01`.

### Wave 16 — the guards learn to lose

The wave's one testable question, handed to every builder: **what would make this fix silently
un-fixable, and does anything red when I do it?** Wave 15 caught the epic's own instrument certifying
a state it was not measuring; wave 16 closes the class that produced it — a fix whose only guard is a
sentence, and an instrument structurally unable to see the thing it certifies.

**The headline reframes (D184).** The tier floor is not unprovable; it is unwired, and the render leg
is not a floor guard. So slice 1 ships a PAIR — a browserless byte-pin in the already-wired node-20
job that refuses the textual tidy-down at any value below 230, and a third node-22 render job that
refuses the geometric regression at ≤185 — plus the five-tier seam (D185), whose justification is
rewritten from "the floor is unprovable" (false) to "the floor's VALUE is unguarded from ~186 to 230
and at every width above 901" (measured). Both halves state which mutation they refuse, and every PR
this wave says **advisory** and means it (D186).

**Four person-facing slices, honestly re-derived, none padded.** `cch-w14-bl-fleet-support-row-169px-band`
is PAID on main and closes by measurement rather than counting as a slot (D189), and two of the wish's
three named ratio candidates are a CORPUS row and an INSTRUMENT row — said out loud rather than
counted. The four that survive are the two pill rows shipped as one slice (D188), the ungated Visit
affordance (D194), the truncated billing chip above 768, and the 900-rule drop (D193), which pays
28 measured defects → 1 and subsumes the 320 page-scroll row.

**Two things the wave does NOT build.** The stamp row is refuted twice and shipped fixed 26h before it
was filed (D187) — it closes by measurement, with its PDS twin. And the split is not re-derived: the
instruments charter stays stranded on #8500, and Law 0's destination is restated as the TASK id, which
is legal today and discharges two of three criteria on `cch-w12-bl-filing-law-parent-charter-half`
without touching that PR.

**Rounds are law, and the app.css train is single-file.** Round 1 is four disjoint slices; round 2 is
four deferrals the lead dispatches after merges, in a strict order set by two shared artifacts:
`breakpoint-sweep.mjs` (s2 → s7 → s8) and `cssom-heads.baseline`, which collided FIVE times in one
session and is REGENERATE-ON-REBASE by D158 — s3 → s6 → s8, each re-measuring on the merged tree,
never resolving.

the wave derives itself, behind a generator that can no longer lose a committed name or promote an
excluded aggregator's leaves, a rehearsed two-command rollback, and a committed open-PR deadlock sweep
that refuses the flip if a currently-mergeable PR would brick. Weight 2 gets real mass, not leftovers:
the cold-boot payload stops shipping 1.16 MB uncompressed, and the last lying destroy verb stops
reporting success over a list that never moved. Weight 3 gets said plainly: the seal is REFUSED with all
three clause letters UNEVALUATED on 84 open rows, and the flip changes that by exactly nothing — what it
changes is that clause (b) CAN pass, provably, for the first time in the epic's life.

**D95 — guerrilla's task CREATE verb was DOWN at Decide, and the wave adapted rather than stalled.**
Measured, with a control: `create` on a minimal four-field task doc returned **HTTP 500
`internal_error` at 27.7s**, reproducibly, across the `bp` CLI (31s client timeout) and raw curl at
240s; the identical-shaped `patch` on an existing task returned **HTTP 200 in 0.49s**, and a 160KB
paper patch also returned 200. So it is the CREATE path specifically, not payload size and not the
server as a whole — which REGRESSES the survey's "create is HEALTHY, 10/10 HTTP 200" finding within
the same day. Consequences, both honest: Movement 0 is carried by `cch-bl-bands-136-reproduce`, an
existing open row whose own subject IS census reproduction and whose embedded arithmetic was stale a
third time (it asserts 91 children / 85 open against a measured 135/83/41/9/2) — reuse, not a
workaround, and better than minting a duplicate. And **one planned backlog row could not be filed**:

into D88, into the seal-retarget slice's brief as the reason clause (b) fails, and into the wave
Paper — but it has no ledger row yet, and wave 8 must file it the moment `create` recovers.

`__app.test.mjs` at genuinely distant regions — sessions ~`1076-1087`, providers ~`1980-2210`,
overview ~`4931` and ~`12727`; slices 1 and 2 share `router.ex` at `~8322/8377` vs
`652/713/1061/1535/7848/10714`. Grep for anchors at build time; never trust a line number.

**HIGH-FLIP-RISK (E2) — two slices, named for the reviewer.** Slice 1: *"rotation claims no new
authority surface and needs no confirm step"* — a security/authz judgment (D78). Slice 2: *"each of
the six write sites reports its own true origin"* — the `session_opts/1` trap (D80), where a wrong
call makes every test pass while five sites report one answer. Both warrant a genuinely INDEPENDENT
second re-derivation before merge; that dispatch is a manual lead step.

**Movement 2 (Paper spine, no build slice).** The honest seal arithmetic after Movement 0 (~82 → ~69
open), D83's re-parenting requirement, and the `gr-backlog-e02-deploy-actor` ruling: **RATIFY
TRIGGER-ONLY**. Grounds, measured: five code paths create deployments and only TWO write
`site.deploy_requested` (a coalesced manual deploy stamps nothing; promotion writes a different verb;
the GitHub push webhook writes no audit at all), so the join can never attribute more than 2 of 5;
`list_audit_events` has no `target_id IN (…)` filter, so a page of deployments is N+1 or a misaligned
200-row sweep — a new server-side batch filter, not a UI join; it acquires a 90-day retention cliff
the deployment row does not have; and the attribution operators want ALREADY exists, backend-true and
filterable, in Activity (`ACTION_LABELS["site.deploy_requested"]`, the `site.deploy` filter chip, actor
chips built from the member list, falling back to `"system"`). GR27/GR28 ratified trigger-only *for
that wave only*, so this is a genuinely new decision — do not cite them as having made it. If a future
wave wants ladder attribution anyway, the honest build is an `actor_user_id` column on `deployments`
stamped where the write site already holds it and rendered only when present — same rule as D80 — not
the join.

**Handed OFF, deliberately not adopted:** the repo-wide Vercel red is **diagnosed** and belongs to
Honest Gates (`hg-bl-vercel-legacy-statuses-red-repo-wide`, open, unclaimed, 1/8) — adopting it would
recreate the duplicate-ownership defect that cancelling `gr-blk-vercel-checks-ungoverned` cured. The
diagnosis, run with `vercel inspect --logs --scope guerrilla` (the default scope
`frikk-jarls-projects` cannot see these deployments, which is probably why nobody got the log): six
`Module not found` on `@barkpark/react` and `@barkpark/core`, because their entry points are `./dist/*`,
`dist/` is gitignored, and nothing builds the workspace packages before `next build` — diff-independent,
hence red on every sha. The complete fix is `workspace:*` (in-tree precedent:
`apps/mobile/package.json:15-16`) **plus** a `prebuild` in `web/package.json`; `prebuild` alone leaves
2 errors because pnpm INJECTS a copy of `@barkpark/react` (it has peerDependencies + `files:[dist]`)
while `@barkpark/core` is symlinked and heals. Both red checks are two Vercel projects rooted at the
same `web/` directory — deleting one removes a permanent red with no code change.

**Deferred, FILED as backlog:** the `--ok`/`--danger` ruling (D73, row rewritten with the measured
facts, awaits Fable); focus-ring contrast across 5 identities × 2 modes (D76); provider identity echo
on rotation (D78); the seal predicate's epic retarget + its first tests (D84); `smoke.mjs` red on main
and CI-invisible; `console-harness.yml:7` claiming 415 tests for a 699-test suite. Also unchanged and
NOT built: `gr-blk-worktree-registry-bloat` does not reproduce as filed — 1511 registrations, **ZERO**
prunable, all directories present, 260 dirty of which ~170 hold real tracked edits, and the safe
`clean AND merged` predicate bites **18 (1.2%)**. It is a source-side leak (the agent harness never
removes what it creates, growing ~1 entry per few minutes under load) wanting a rewrite, not a build.

**THE WISH'S THREE "MECHANICAL FACTS" WERE ONE-THIRD WRONG, AND TWO BUILDERS CAUGHT IT BY DERIVING.**
The brief said to inherit "four breakpoints (620/720/768/899) giving 12 boundary widths, down from
five/13, because s8 dropped the 900 tier rule". Leg A on merged main prints
`5 breakpoints [620,720,768,830,899] -> 15 boundary widths` — s6's 830 tier is in the axis and s8's
drop did not remove a breakpoint. S3 corrected the harness prose to FIVE/FIFTEEN from the sweep's own
output; S4 re-derived 100/26/25/75/13 from `scenarioReport` rather than copying the brief. The
baseline fact (1252) and the `sites-on-instance` allowlist fact were both correct.
**LAW RESTATED: a number handed down in a brief is L6. Derive it or do not quote it.**

**FOUND AT REVIEW BY DRIVING, AND NOT PAPERED OVER.** S4's scheme fix is production-faithful — and
that fidelity EXPOSES a defect the short fixture had been concealing for the whole epic:
`.instance-card-url` is `overflow:hidden; text-overflow:ellipsis` (app.css:3067-3070) and renders
`publicUrl(bp)` as TEXT, so the real address ellipsises on the FRONT SCREEN. Driven at review:
**7 clipped cells** at 320/360 across both front-screen scenarios (244/288 for
`https://production-5b2c1e.barkpark.cloud` at 320), against **0** on origin/main's bare fixtures.
What is cut is the END — the TLD — so two boxes on different domains can render as the same visible
string. **No committed instrument can see this**: the sweep's Q2 census counts `text-overflow:
ellipsis` as an authored cue and skips the element, and no guard leg names the selector. Filed as
`cch-w18-bl-instance-card-url-ellipsised-on-phone`. The review also finished S4's half-applied sweep
(five bare `url:` fixtures left the front screen printing two address formats side by side) and drove
the guard on it — W15 stays 90/90 with the eight extra characters.

**LEDGER.** S1 criterion 4 was **amended by measurement at review**: as written it demanded a SINGLE
old-length-zero hunk, which is jointly unsatisfiable with criterion 8's same-commit comment
correction. Re-cut to the driven two-hunk shape (`-5323 +5323` and `@@ -5341,0 +5342,79 @@`, neither
range intersecting 2913-2957) — the identical carve-out proof, a satisfiable row. S3's criterion 7
evidence claimed "mixed-fleet renders ZERO `.attention-row`"; that holds only at mixed-fleet's DEFAULT
route — at `#overview` the committed leg prints three (`[att 170/245 139/237 59/59]` at 320). The
CONCLUSION survives (139/237 reproduced to the pixel); the sentence was corrected in place so wave 19
does not inherit it. No task outside this wave was touched.

instrument scanning the file you touched is not a gate — said in wave 16, unenforced in wave 17.**

mutation-test expectation + every width-count sentence moved with it, and the full render leg at the

widths` / `… walked at all 15 widths`.

**Paper:** `cloud-console-hardening-wave-16-2026-08-01`. PRs #8816-#8819. Nothing merged by the wave;

| slice | final branch | PR | verdict |

| s2 sweep axes theme/height/scenario | `…cannot-se-1-r` | #8817 | three axes, refusals in BOTH directions, `--cell a,b,c` with a per-name refusal, and `cch-w15-bl-lega-cannot-refuse-removed-breakpoint` folded in. Suite 32 → 51. |

| s5 stale-open audit | `…both-direction-3` | #8819 | 13+ rows adjudicated on CRITERIA in both directions; 9 closed, 2 cancelled as refuted, 2 drafts discarded, 1 deliberately left open at 2/3. |

**THE PAIR IS JUSTIFIED BY MEASUREMENT, NOT BY TASTE — re-derived by the reviewer.** A 200px floor
passes the FULL 13-width render leg **exit 0** while the byte-pin fails naming "measured 200px". So
the render half is a `<=185` guard and the byte-pin a text guard; **neither subsumes the other**, and
the 186..229 band is guarded ONLY textually. All three halves were re-driven at 180 (byte-pin fails;
render reds `149 > 141 "Yours when the trial ends"`; `--tiers5` reds `3trk@185px` at 901 and
`5trk@180.391px` at 1700) and `app.css` restored clean.

**TWO REVIEWER FIXES, AND BOTH ARE THE WAVE'S OWN DISEASE.** (1) s2 renames the bare module const
`HEIGHT` to `RENDER_HEIGHT`; s1's new `--tiers5` leg reads it. The two branches merge **CLEANLY** and
then die at run time: `ReferenceError: HEIGHT is not defined` — no conflict marker, no test, and
`--tiers5` is wired to no job, so nothing in CI would have said so. Fixed by giving the leg its own
`TIERS5_HEIGHT`. (2) s2's three new `app.js:<line>` citations trip `__css_check` **E11 — 3 error(s)**
— and `__css_check` runs in the WIRED `console-unit` job, so the merge PR would have reded. The
slice's own gate did not include `__css_check` even though it edits a file `__css_check` scans.
**A gate that does not include every instrument scanning the file you touched is not a gate.**

**The ratio was met at the PLAN, and discharged honestly.** Of the wish's three named candidates only
one is person-facing; `-detail-url-fixture-never-overflows` is a CORPUS row (240 == 240, truncated
false in every cell) and `-cuestuck-…` is an INSTRUMENT row (the cue is correctly live; the sweep's
note is wrong). The four that survive are s3 (paying TWO rows), s4, s6, s8 — **three of them are
round-2 and did not fly this run.** Round 1 therefore shipped ONE person-facing slice; the ratio is
paid only when s4/s6/s8 land, and the next wave must not treat it as already banked.

**Merge order matters and is now cheap.** After the reviewer's anchor move, all four branches merge
into `origin/main` with **zero conflicts** in any order, and the merged tree was driven green across
the union of every gate: `__css_check` 0 errors · `__app.test.mjs` 758/758 · `breakpoint-sweep.test`
51/51 · `seal-predicate.test` 49/49 · `smoke` 99/99 · `overflow-guard` PASS · `cssom-parity` PASS
(1253 == baseline 1253) · `--tiers5` exit 0. **`cssom-heads.baseline` is 1253 after s3** — s6 and s8
must re-measure on the merged tree with a real Chrome, never by arithmetic.

**Cost the lead may want to reverse:** s2's theme axis DOUBLES Leg B (676 cells, ~15 min) for an axis
the file itself calls coverage rather than yield. `--theme light` slices it back; defaulting Leg B to
light and making `--theme dark` explicit keeps every refusal at zero cost.

**What this wave proves about instruments, and it is the durable lesson.** On a tree carrying the
fleet-row defect the W13 leg prints `108 / 108 cells clean` and exits 0 while the new W15 leg exits 1
with 12 findings on the identical bytes. **A page-level assertion cannot see a cell that spills
INSIDE the page** — `.fleet-url` computes `overflow: visible`, so 58% of a hostname was painted
THROUGH the badge chips without moving `documentElement.scrollWidth` by one pixel. Both legs were
re-run by the reviewer on the same mutated scratch tree.

while the driven margin is 4px at every height; it now parses `.topbar { height: … }` out of the
shipped bytes, so a taller topbar reds the cheap unit leg (56 → 72px ⇒ exit 1). (2) s4 PROVED that
its two declarations unscoped ship an 846px page scrollWidth at 721/769 and then guarded that with a
comment; the W15 leg now drives `fleet-support-failed` and 721/769 and reads page scrollWidth —
dropping the media query reds it at `846 > 721`, 125px off-screen, both themes.

support row truncates `.status-pill-detail` 463 → 295/343 — the operator cannot read WHY the instance
failed. `cch-w15-bl-fleet-support-detail-truncated-stacked-band`. The allowlist entry names the row,
scenario, selector and exactly two widths; **an entry matching NOTHING is itself a failure**
(mutation-proven), so paying the row reds the guard until the entry is deleted.

`bp task ready` would have offered them for rebuilding; re-claimed to `in_progress`. The four backlog
rows the wave PAID were left at 0 stamped; criteria stamped from re-driven runs, with an honest

(`cch-w15-probe-publishwall-20260801`, s5's criteria verbatim at 0/8) cancelled. Two new rows filed:
the support-row truncation above, and `cch-w15-bl-stamp-merger-silently-drops-evidence` — **two
independent builders in this one wave saw `bp task stamp` print a success receipt and read back
`met=false`**, and neither filed it.

**Merge order.** Four slices touch `app.css` and three bump `cssom-heads.baseline` to three different
numbers (1244, 1245, 1245). They merge CLEAN and WRONG. **Re-measure on the merged tree (D158); do
not add deltas.** No slice registers anything; `.github/required-checks.json` untouched.

### 2026-08-01 — wave 15 DECIDE (build in flight) — 8 slices, five of them a person's screen, and the instrument stops certifying what it does not measure

**Wave Paper:** `cloud-console-hardening-wave-15-2026-07-31`. Charter PR is docs-only; the Paper carries

@H=667** against the sweep's own un-pinned budget of 320 / 266.8, and the ONLY reason the sweep
reports green is `NAV_WALL_PIN.maxTop = 745.88` — **2.27x the measurement**, reds only if it GROWS.

independent verifiers reproduced it.

**But the bar was a deliverable, and BOTH candidate bars died (D174).** `FOLD_FRACTION = 0.4` was
authored whole in one commit citing a measured 56px it sits 5.7x above; S2's `45%` appears ZERO times
in its own brief and was authored by the wave-14 decider AFTER the fix it blesses. 0.4 fails the
shipped shape at every height; 0.45 fails landscape 720x390. The ruling is a shape that makes the bar
an IDENTITY — `calc(40vh - 60px)` gives `contentTop = 0.4H - 4` by construction at every height, one
declaration, zero rule heads, Q3 → 0 including landscape. The pin dies by DELETION (two whole tests,
six assertions, the import binding, the accumulator and the report clause — deleting the export alone
is a link error that executes 0 of 28 tests) **and the same diff must ship a replacement assertion**,
because after an honest deletion nothing pins the bar at all: mutation-proven, `FOLD_FRACTION = 0.99`
goes green everywhere and silences the very reds this wave exists to expose.

**Three of the direction's premises were refuted and the wave follows the evidence.** #8656 and #8657
are MERGED, not open — movement C bases on `origin/main` and its 108/108 criterion is satisfiable
there. `cssom-heads.baseline` is **1243**, not 1241. And `cch-w14-bl-navwall-worse-on-operator-tokens`
is ALREADY PAID (D176): 60+ driven cells read the identical 328 / 282.77 on operator, operator-halted,
tokens-member and overview-fleet, and even the UNCAPPED wall is 185px below the allowance — the pin
went stale by MERGE ORDER (siblings, 45 seconds apart), not by width. **The ratio is refilled BY
CONTENT, named not padded:** `cch-w14-bl-site-open-phone-overflow`, remedy-disjoint from the shell.

**Both "the sweep pays for itself" rows were mis-diagnosed in their own briefs and are amended by
measurement before a builder claims them.** The archives chip FITS (337==337) — the defect is a rigid
parent, the magnitude is **171px at 320, not 10px at 721**, it is fixture-bound by 2.2x against a
63-char product-maximum slug, and completing the brief's implied fix takes Q1 green while cutting 70%
of a shell command (D177). The billing CTA is not a boundary defect at all — it reds to 1700+ and
freezes, because `.content` caps at 1040px, so a 4K monitor shows the same cut label as a 901px window

folded shell, the archives band, the billing CTA, the fleet lead column, and the phone-width site

**Wave Paper:** `cloud-console-hardening-wave-14-2026-07-31`. The wish asked to finish a 621-768

width" sentence is refuted by its OWN row's 336-page-load addendum, and `app.css:4241` is line-stale
(D164). What IS wrong inside the wish's band is VERTICAL and total: `.content` begins at **745.88px**
on every top-level view at every width 621-720 in both themes, and the adjudication the filed row
demanded resolves AGAINST the shell — no skip link, no hamburger, no toggle, 17 of 34 focusables
before the first control in `.content`, and a nav click that swaps the view without moving the page
(D166).

Six slices, all round 1, five of them on a row where **a person looking at the screen is
misinformed** — nav wall, `#fleet` band, rail select, planned rail, preview freshness — against the
wish's floor of four. The sixth is the continuous sweep, filed under `cch-instruments-epic` because
Law 0 says instrument residue belongs to the successor and wave 13 broke that law four times (D172).
**The money minute's remainder was re-derived by content and yields ONE clean survivor plus
preview-freshness; the scrub row is refused BY NAME rather than used to pad the ratio, with the
proof that it has no driveable surface (D171).**

anywhere`, takes the `#fleet` sweep to 0/140 and **shreds hostnames to one character per line**,

hand-copied enum whose stated grep returns nothing cannot be re-verified, which is the only thing the

driving 18 adversarial strings through `FailureCopy.scrub/1`: five ordinary failure strings were

expired"`, `"missing Bearer credentials"`, `"no api_key: set in the config file"`, `"password:
missing"`. A redaction where no secret was is the same class of lie the wave is paying off, so a
`\b`-anchored `@prose_value` stop-list now guards the value position of both key-bearing clauses; it
costs the scrub zero coverage (every positive still redacts, including `client_secret=timeout`), five
negative rows were added, and removing the guard reds exactly two of them.

### 2026-07-31 — wave 12 DECIDE (build in flight) — 6 slices, all round 1, and the ratio finally inverts

**Wave Paper:** `cloud-console-hardening-wave-12-2026-07-31`. **Charter PR:** docs-only, opened from a
worktree cut from `origin/main` — the primary checkout never leaves `main`.

**THE RATIO INVERTS, MEASURED.** Waves 9/10/11 spent 6-of-6, 4-of-6 and 2-of-4 slices on instruments.
Wave 12 spends **4 of 6 on rows where a person looking at the screen is misinformed**, one on the
epic's own arithmetic, one on ledger truth. Nothing on gates, generators, specs or registration.

**WHAT VERIFICATION CHANGED, AND IT CHANGED A LOT.** Twelve verifiers flew and the direction did not
survive intact. Two of the five named lies were already PAID on `origin/main` (D142); the digest's
headline "new lie nobody had filed" was REFUTED by a real Tab press (D142); the ember ok/danger
collapse is real at the token layer and paints nothing a person can be misled by (D143); and the
estimates row had to be re-scoped off the rail the direction chose, because the provision cohort is
FOUR jobs while the deploy cohort is 8211 (D145). **Two of five candidate lies did not survive first
contact with a browser, and both had been "confirmed" by reading — the wave's own evidence bar,
turned on the wave.** The four that DID survive driving are D144, and they are the four slices.

**THE SLICES.** S1 the Activity Who-axis cold-boot latch (`app.js`); S2 narrow-viewport truth — the
page body's phone-width horizontal scroll plus the notifications matrix's unsignalled clipping
(`app.css`); S3 the Sites-card focus perimeter, three of four sides unpainted (`app.css`, disjoint
region); S4 deploy-rail estimates from measured medians (`registry.ex`, `router.ex`, `app.js`); S5 the
letterbox fence plus the scope split into a REAL successor (`seal-predicate.mjs` + a new charter); S6
ledger truth — six free closes, two overrides, five duplicate families collapsed, one stale comment
edited (`accounts.ex`). All six are round 1 and dependency-free. S1 and S4 co-occupy `app.js` in
disjoint regions (~11014/12290-12332/16744-16874 vs ~10249/13387-13460), as do S2 and S3 in `app.css`
(~1876-1940 and ~2745-2870 vs ~4326); briefs name the boundaries so a rebase stays trivial.

**THE RULING (D146–D148).** The epic does not terminate as chartered, and the reason is structural:
vision rows are terminal, instrument rows are generative, and 79.3% of the live roster is instrument
by BODY. The mechanism is a scope split into a real successor — but the letterbox must be fenced
FIRST (D147: a DONE row that is a CHILD of this epic resolves as a legal forwarding address today),
and **no predicate run is quoted as a verdict this wave** (D148). The seal stays honestly refused for
a fifth wave. Seven registration-class rows stay put, because moving them would relocate an unmade
human decision.

**WHAT THE WAVE FILED, SAID OUT LOUD.** Fourteen rows: six slices and eight backlog rows, taking the
roster from 189 children to 203 before a single close. Under standing law 0 that debt is S5's and
S6's to clear — S6 disposes of roughly ten rows by content and S5 moves ~66 out — and if the wave ends
larger than it began, it broke its own new law and the debrief must say so in those words.

**FABLE WAS UNAVAILABLE.** Every slice is declared `opus`, including the two visually-designed CSS
slices that would otherwise warrant `fable@high`. That is a constraint on this wave, not a judgement
about the work: S2 and S3 are judged against the Kinsta/Vercel bar and their briefs say so.
